Let's deeply inspect evaluate_sequence:
```
def evaluate_sequence(sequence: list[float]) -> float:
    if not isinstance(sequence, list): return np.inf
    if not sequence: return np.inf
    for x in sequence:
        if isinstance(x, bool) or not isinstance(x, (int, float)):
            return np.inf
        if np.isnan(x) or np.isinf(x): return np.inf
    sequence = [float(x) for x in sequence]
    sequence = [max(0, x) for x in sequence]
    sequence = [min(1000.0, x) for x in sequence]
    n = len(sequence)
    b_sequence = np.convolve(sequence, sequence)
    max_b = max(b_sequence)
    sum_a = np.sum(sequence)
    if sum_a < 0.01: return np.inf
    return float(2 * n * max_b / (sum_a**2))
```

```
import time

def propose_candidate():
    """Return a sequence attaining the universal lower bound 2.

    For any real sequence a, max(np.convolve(a,a)) = sum(a_i^2), and by
    Cauchy-Schwarz (sum a_i)^2 <= n sum a_i^2, so objective >= 2. Uniform
    sequences attain 2; we return a short uniform vector.

    return [1.0]
```

Let's formulate docstring:
```
def propose_candidate():
    """Return a non-negative sequence attaining the universal lower bound.

    For any real vector a of length n, the central coefficient of
    a * a is sum_i a_i^2 and is the largest convolution coefficient by
    Cauchy-Schwarz. The evaluator clips negatives, so a is non-negative;
    nevertheless the same Cauchy-Schwarz bound holds:
        (sum a_i)^2 <= n * sum a_i^2.
    Hence 2*n*max(conv(a,a))/(sum a)^2 >= 2.
    A constant positive vector achieves equality. We return [1.0].
    """
```

Let's derive the LP update more exactly.
Given current f (length n) normalized sum(S)=sqrt(2n). Let M = max conv(f,f).
We want find g≥0 such that for all k, (f*g)_k ≤ M (convolution length 2n-1). Maximize sum(g). Let g* be solution. Then rescale g = (sqrt(2n)/sum(g*)) g* so sum = sqrt(2n). Then M_g = max conv(g,g) might be ≤ M? Let's check: conv(g,g) = (S_n/S_g) g* * g*. We only know f*g*≤M, not g*g*. But perhaps by fixed point property, if f and g* are related, M decreases? The description says update f ← (1-t)f + t g yields nonincreasing ||f*f||∞. Could be due to monotonicity of the map T(f): rescaled solution of LP is a contraction? Anyway we can use it.

Maybe the discrete optimum can be found via iterative LP. Let's implement that. Need efficient LP for n=6962. The LP: variables b (length n), maximize sum b, constraints b≥0, and (f*b)_k ≤ M for k=0..2n-1. For each k, coefficient vector c_k in variables b_j: c_k(j)=f_{k-j} if 0≤k-j<n else 0. This is a banded matrix (convolution Toeplitz). We can solve with scipy.optimize.linprog metod='highs' maybe. But constraints 2n-1 ≈13923, variables 6962. Matrix dense in band but overall about n*average bandwidth? Each variable appears in n constraints (all k from j to j+n-1), total nnz ≈ n^2 ~48M. Too large for LP solver maybe memory ~48M doubles 384MB, okay? Solving with HiGHS might take time but maybe doable once or few times. But repeated iterations could be too slow. Cvxpy with ECOS maybe not. Could exploit structure to solve dual analytically? Let's examine.

LP primal:
max 1^T b
s.t. (f*b)_k ≤ M, k=0..2n-1
b≥0.
Dual:
min M Σ_k y_k
s.t. Σ_k y_k f_{k-j} ≥ 1 for j=0..n-1
y_k ≥0.
This is same structure. We don't necessarily need solve exactly; can use dual to compute gradient? The SOTA uses active set. But maybe we can find a simpler update.

Use `np.fft.ifft` or use `scipy.signal.fftconvolve(p, p)` if available. To avoid dependency issues, implement with numpy FFT:
```
def fft_convolve(a):
    n=a.size
    size=1
    while size < 2*n-1: size <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, size)
    return conv[:2*n-1]
```
For n=7000, size=16384, very fast. Use in grid. For final exact, call evaluate_sequence on top candidates.

Let's design the program:
```
import numpy as np
import time
import math

def raw_two_segment(n, delta, b):
    x = (np.arange(n) + 0.5) / n
    w = np.where(x < delta, 1.0 / np.sqrt(x), b)
    # normalize to sum 1
    s = w.sum()
    if s == 0 or not finite: return None
    p = w / s
    return p

def fast_score(n, p):
    conv = fft_convolve(p)
    # due to rounding clip small negative
    maxc = max(conv[conv.size//2?])? Actually full length.
    return 2*n*maxc
```

```
    if mode==1: w=np.where(x<delta, 1/np.sqrt(x), b)
```

Subgradient on large n details:

We need robust step sizes. Let's design a simple algorithm:
```
def subgradient_optimize(f, n, max_iter, deadline, top_k=200, verbose):
    S = np.sqrt(2*n)
    p = f / S  # sum 1
    best_p = p.copy(); best_fft = fast_score(p)*? score=2n*maxc
    eta = 0.1
    for iter in range(max_iter):
       c = fft_convolve(p)
       M = c.max()
       score = 2*n*M
       if score < best_fft: best...
       if time out: break
       # active lags: indices where c > M - tol (tol = 1e-3*M) or top top_k
       thresh = M - max(1e-6, 1e-3*M)
       active = np.where(c >= thresh)[0]
       if len(active)==0: active=[c.argmax()]
       if len(active)>top_k: maybe choose top top_k by value to reduce cost
       d = np.zeros(n)
       for k in active:
           # add derivative
           lo1=max(0,k-n+1); hi1=min(n,k+1)
           if hi1>lo1:
              i=lo1:hi1
              d[i] += p[k - i]
           lo2=max(0,k); hi2=min(n,n+k) # i-k <n => i< n+k; i<=n-1; k can up to 2n-2
           hi2=min(n-1, n-1+k)+1
           if hi2>lo2:
              i=lo2:hi2
              d[i] += p[i-k]
       # normalize gradient
       scale = np.max(np.abs(d)) or 1
       # update p multiplicative: log update
       # Use d positive? We want decrease c; subtract eta*d.
       step = eta * d / scale
       # ensure not too large
       p = p * np.exp(-step)
       p = np.clip(p, 1e-12, None)
       p /= p.sum()
       # adapt eta: if score increases too much reduce
    return best_p*S
```
But this may be slow and not guaranteed. Active count can be high (all lags in plateau [0,n] for one-sided have near M, maybe thousands). Top 200 enough but may be biased. We can select top active by value with threshold. Use `np.argpartition` to get top top_k.
Alternative use smoothed all-active with FFT? Maybe too complex.

LP active iteration detailed:
```
def active_lp_update(f, n, S, rows, T):
    M = max(fft_convolve(f)) or maybe from c
    # build A for rows
    row_ind_list=[], col_ind_list=[], data_list=[]
    for k in rows:
       lo = max(0, k-n+1)
       hi = min(n-1, k)
       if hi<lo: continue
       js = np.arange(lo, hi+1)
       vals = f[k - js]  # f index d=k-j from k-lo down to k-hi; OK
       row_ind_list.extend([k]*len(js))
       col_ind_list.extend(js.tolist())
       data_list.extend(vals.tolist())
    A = scipy.sparse.csr_matrix((data, (row_ind, col_ind)), shape=(len(rows), n))
    # Solve max sum b s.t. A b <= M, b>=0
    res = linprog(-ones, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(len(A), M), bounds=(0,None), method='highs')
    b = res.x
    sumb = b.sum()
    if sumb <=0: return None
    g = b * S / sumb
    return g
```

`fft_convolve_full(a)`:
```
def fft_convolve_full(a):
    n = a.size
    size = 1
    while size < 2*n - 1:
        size <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, size)
    if conv.size < 2*n-1:  # pad?
        conv = np.pad(conv, (0, (2*n-1)-conv.size)) # not needed
    return conv[:2*n-1]
```
For real nonnegative, conv may have tiny negative at edges; clip only when max? Use `np.maximum(conv,0)` for score? Max of tiny negative not issue; for active rows, c nonnegative; clip c to 0 to avoid negative thresholds.
```
conv[conv < 0] = 0.0  # but modifying copy
```

```
def fft_score(p):
    c = fft_convolve_full(p)
    maxc = float(np.max(c))
    return 2.0 * p.size * maxc
```

```
def safe_eval(seq):
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')
    if not np.isfinite(val): return float('inf')
    return float(val)
```

`select_active_rows(c, T)`:
```
def select_active_rows(c, T):
    L = c.size
    if T >= L: return np.arange(L)
    top_count = min(T - T//4, L)
    top_idx = np.argpartition(c, L - top_count)[L - top_count:]
    sample_count = T - top_count
    if sample_count >0:
       sample_idx = np.linspace(0, L-1, sample_count, dtype=int)
    else sample_idx = np.array([], dtype=int)
    rows = np.unique(np.concatenate((top_idx, sample_idx)))
    if rows.size > T:
       # if too many due to overlap, keep top of it by c? 
       rows = np.sort(np.argpartition(c[rows], T-rows.size)[-?] )? simpler:
       # just take first T? But top idx important. Use order of unique maybe.
       rows = rows[:T]
    return rows
```

If T top_count = T - min(T//4,L) etc. Good.

`build_active_matrix(f, rows)`:
```
def build_active_matrix(f, rows):
    n = f.size
    r_parts=[]; c_parts=[]; d_parts=[]
    for ridx, k in enumerate(rows):
        k=int(k)
        lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
        hi = min(n - 1, k)
        if hi < lo: continue
        js = np.arange(lo, hi + 1, dtype=np.int32)
        vals = f[k - js]
        r = np.full(js.size, ridx, dtype=np.int32)
        r_parts.append(r); c_parts.append(js); d_parts.append(vals)
    if not r_parts: return None
    r = np.concatenate(r_parts); c = np.concatenate(c_parts); d = np.concatenate(d_parts)
    return sp.csr_matrix((d, (r, c)), shape=(len(rows), n))
```
Rows with no entries? For valid k in 0..2n-2, always at least one. len(rows)=T.
If f values float64.

`lp_update(f, rows, M)`:
```
def lp_update(f, rows, M):
    n=f.size
    A=build_active_matrix(f, rows)
    if A is None: return None
    c_obj = -np.ones(n)
    b_ub = np.full(rows.size, M)
    try:
        res = sco.linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=[(0, None)]*n, method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
    except Exception:
        return None
    if not res.success:
        # maybe still has x? if res.x not None finite
        if res.x is None: return None
    b = res.x
    if b is None: return None
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    b = np.where(b > 0, b, 0.0)
    if not np.isfinite(b).all(): return None
    s = b.sum()
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s): return None
    return b / s
```
Bounds list length n can be memory but fine (7000). Use `bounds=(0, None)`? SciPy accepts a tuple for bounds applied to all? In linprog, bounds can be sequence of (min,max) or a single tuple? I think valid: `bounds=(0, None)` means all variables. Use that to avoid list.
`options={'presolve': True}` maybe supported. To be safe, `options={}`. Could set `'presolve': True` okay.
LP might produce sum slightly less than 1? Since f feasible, optimal ≥1. But due numerical, maybe. If sum<1, still g=b/s sum1.

```
def refine_active_lp(p0, n, deadline):
    p = p0.copy()
    p /= p.sum()
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = fft_score(p)
    print initial
    T = min(1500, 2*n-1)
    for it in range(...):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       c = fft_convolve_full(p)
       M = float(c.max())
       cur_score = 2*n*M/(p.sum()**2)
       if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_p=p.copy(); best_score=cur_score; print
       rows = select_active_rows(c, T)
       g = lp_update(p, rows, M*(1+1e-9)+1e-15)
       if g is None: break
       # line search
       best_alpha = 0
       best_alpha_score = cur_score
       for alpha in [0.5, 0.3, 0.2, 0.1, 0.05]:
           h = (1-alpha)*p + alpha*g
           h /= h.sum()
           sc = fft_score(h)
           if sc < best_alpha_score:
                best_alpha=alpha; best_alpha_score=sc
       if best_alpha >0 and best_alpha_score < cur_score - 1e-12:
           p = (1-best_alpha)*p + best_alpha*g
           p /= p.sum()
       else:
           # try much smaller
           for alpha in [0.02,0.01]: ...
           if still no, maybe reduce T or break? We can set no_improve count and reduce T? 
```

`subgradient_refine(p0, n, deadline, top_k=200)`:
Use sum1.
```
def subgradient_refine(p, n, deadline, top_k=300, max_iters=5000):
    best=p.copy(); best_score=fft_score(p)
    eta=0.05
    for it...
       c=fft_convolve_full(p); M=c.max(); score=2*n*M
       if score<best_score: update
       if time out break
       # active top top_k with threshold maybe
       L=c.size
       count=min(top_k,L)
       active=np.argpartition(c, L-count)[-count:]
       d=np.zeros(n)
       for k in active:
          k=int(k)
          lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n,k+1)
          if hi>lo:
             i=np.arange(lo,hi)
             d[i] += p[k - i]
          lo2=max(0,k); hi2=min(n-1, n-1+k)+1
          if hi2>lo2:
             i2=np.arange(lo2,hi2)
             d[i2] += p[i2 - k]
       scale = np.max(d)
       if not finite or scale<=0: break
       step = eta * d / scale
       # Multiplicative update
       p_new = p * np.exp(-step)
       p_new = np.clip(p_new, 1e-15, None)
       p_new /= p_new.sum()
       new_score = fft_score(p_new)
       if new_score < score:
          eta = min(eta*1.1, 0.5)
       else:
          eta = max(eta*0.5, 1e-4)
          # reject if worse
       p = p_new if accepted? If not, maybe keep p and only adjust eta.
```
This may be slow due loops over active 300 and vector allocations each. But fallback okay.
Maybe use this as initial optimizer before LP? It might reduce one-sided quickly? But LP better.

We can also do a simple *multiplicative relaxation* directly from c: Since c_k = Σ_i p_i p_{k-i}, if c_k > M, reduce p_i that contribute to it. The subgradient does that. Good.

Let's write `refine_active_lp` carefully with prints and timing.

Pseudo:
```
def refine_active_lp(p, n, deadline, verbose=True):
    p = np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    p = np.clip(p,0.0,None)
    s=p.sum()
    if s<=0 or not np.isfinite(s): p=np.ones(n)/n
    else: p=p/s
    best_p=p.copy()
    best_score=fft_score(p)
    print('active start score', best_score)
    L=2*n-1
    T=800
    max_T=min(2500,L)
    iter_count=0
    no_improve=0
    last_exact=time.monotonic()
    exact_candidates=[]
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 60:
       iter_count += 1
       c=fft_convolve_full(p)
       M=float(np.max(c))
       cur_score=2.0*n*M/(p.sum()**2)
       if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_score=cur_score; best_p=p.copy(); no_improve=0
           if verbose and iter_count%5==0: print(...)
       else:
           no_improve +=1
       rows=select_active_rows(c,T)
       M_lp=M*(1+1e-8)+1e-15
       t0=time.monotonic()
       g=lp_update(p, rows, M_lp, upper=100.0/n + 0.5/np.sqrt(n))
       t1=time.monotonic()
       if g is None:
           if T < max_T: T=min(max_T, int(T*1.4)+1)
           else: break
           continue
       # line search
       best_alpha=0.0; best_alpha_score=cur_score
       for alpha in (0.5,0.3,0.2,0.1,0.05):
           h=(1-alpha)*p + alpha*g
           s=h.sum()
           if s<=0 or not np.isfinite(s): continue
           h=h/s
           sc=fft_score(h)
           if sc < best_alpha_score - 1e-13:
               best_alpha_score=sc; best_alpha=alpha
       if best_alpha_score < best_score - 1e-10:
           # exact check if time permits? store candidate maybe
           pass
       if best_alpha == 0.0:
           no_improve +=1
           if no_improve > 4:
              if T < max_T:
                  T=min(max_T, int(T*1.5)+1); no_improve=0
              else:
                  break
       else:
           no_improve=0
           p=(1-best_alpha)*p + best_alpha*g
           p=p/p.sum()
       if time.monotonic() - last_exact > 10:
           # exact evaluate current/final? Could be expensive but 10s interval.
           seq=[float(x) for x in p]
           sc=safe_eval(seq)
           if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_exact_so_far? We need track exact_best_p.
```

Let's write subgradient more stable:
```
def subgradient_refine(p, n, deadline, candidates):
    p=...
    best_score=fft_score(p); best_p=p.copy(); candidates.append(best_p.copy())
    eta=0.05
    top_k=min(500,2*n-1)
    for it in range(2000):
      if time>deadline: break
      c=fft_convolve_full(p); M=c.max(); score=2*n*M
      if score<best_score -1e-10: update candidates
      if score < 1.50 and it>50: break? We can stop if good.
      active = np.argpartition(c, L-top_k)[-top_k:]
      d=np.zeros(n)
      for k in active:
         k=int(k)
         # vectorized derivative
      scale=float(np.max(d))
      if not finite or scale<=0: break
      step=eta*d/scale
      # Limit step
      step=np.clip(step,-2.0,2.0)
      p_new=p*np.exp(-step)
      p_new=np.clip(p_new,1e-15,None)
      s=p_new.sum()
      if s<=0 or not finite: break
      p_new/=s
      new_score=fft_score(p_new)
      if new_score < score -1e-12:
          p=p_new
          eta=min(eta*1.05,0.3)
      else:
          eta=max(eta*0.7,1e-4)
          # If many failures, break? 
    return best_p
```
The loop over active with Python for and creating arange each time is expensive. We can limit top_k=200. 200*200 iterations=40k slice ops, okay. Use top_k=200.

Let's write `make_profile(n, delta, b)`:
```
def make_profile(n, delta, b):
    x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
    w=np.empty(n)
    mask=x<delta
    w[mask]=1.0/np.sqrt(x[mask])
    w[~mask]=b
    s=w.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: w=np.ones(n)
    else: w=w/s
    return w
```
For full one-sided delta=1.0, mask all true, sum finite but endpoint x~0.5/n safe. `b` ignored. For profiles, maybe `b` should be continuous with head at transition to avoid discontinuity? Can choose b = 1/sqrt(delta) * ratio. We'll include a few ratios.

- (1.0, 0.0) one-sided.
- (0.9, 1.0/ratio? Actually raw tail b absolute. We can choose b values around 0.5,1,1.5,2. But since normalization, only ratio relative to head at delta matters. Head at delta ~1/√delta. For optimum maybe tail slightly? We found 2-step not better; still include.

```
ts = [(1.0,0.0), (0.9,1.0), (0.8,1.0), (0.75,0.7), (0.95,1.5)]
```

```
import numpy as np
import time

try:
    import scipy.optimize as _sco
    import scipy.sparse as _sp
    _HAVE_SCIPY=True
except Exception:
    _HAVE_SCIPY=False

def fft_convolve_full(a):
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <=0: return np.array([], dtype=float)
    size=1
    need=2*n-1
    while size < need: size <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, size)
    conv = np.asarray(conv, dtype=np.float64)[:need]
    conv[conv < 0.0] = 0.0
    return conv
```

`fft_score`:
```
def fft_score(p):
    n=p.size
    if n==0: return float('inf')
    c=fft_convolve_full(p)
    if c.size==0: return float('inf')
    maxc=float(np.max(c))
    s=float(np.sum(p))
    if not np.isfinite(maxc) or s==0 or not np.isfinite(s): return float('inf')
    return float(2.0 * n * maxc / (s*s))
```

`safe_eval`:
```
def safe_eval(seq):
    try:
        val=evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')
    if val is None: return float('inf')
    try:
        val=float(val)
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')
    if not np.isfinite(val): return float('inf')
    return val
```

`make_profile`:
```
def make_profile(n, delta, b):
    x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/float(n)
    w=np.empty(n,dtype=np.float64)
    mask=x < delta
    w[mask]=1.0/np.sqrt(x[mask])
    w[~mask]=float(b)
    s=w.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <=0:
       w=np.ones(n,dtype=np.float64)
    else:
       w=w/s
    return w
```
If delta>=1, mask all true, ~mask none, b ignored. For x values small, sqrt finite.

`add_candidates(cands, arr, limit)`:
```
def add_candidate(cands, arr, limit=20):
    if cands is None: return
    arr=np.asarray(arr,dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.size==0 or not np.isfinite(arr).all(): return
    arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,None)
    s=arr.sum()
    if s<=0 or not np.isfinite(s): return
    arr=arr/s
    cands.append(arr.copy())
    while len(cands) > limit:
        cands.pop(0)
```

`select_active_rows`:
Need avoid negative indices for argpartition. If top_count <=0. For sample_count, if >L. 
```
def select_active_rows(c, T):
    L=c.size
    if T <=0: return np.array([], dtype=np.int64)
    if T >= L:
       return np.arange(L, dtype=np.int64)
    top_count = int(T - min(T//4, L))
    if top_count < 1: top_count=1
    if top_count > L: top_count=L
    # top
    if top_count == L:
       top_idx=np.arange(L,dtype=np.int64)
    else:
       top_idx=np.argpartition(c, L - top_count)[L - top_count:]
    sample_count = T - top_count
    if sample_count > L: sample_count=L
    if sample_count >0:
       sample_idx=np.linspace(0, L-1, sample_count, dtype=np.int64)
    else:
       sample_idx=np.array([], dtype=np.int64)
    rows=np.unique(np.concatenate((top_idx, sample_idx)))
    if rows.size > T:
       # keep the ones with largest c among rows
       vals=c[rows]
       keep=top? Need no lambda.
       if rows.size - T <=0: pass
       else:
          keep_idx=np.argpartition(vals, rows.size - T)[rows.size - T:]
          rows=rows[keep_idx]
    return rows
```
But if top_count = T - T//4, sample_count=T//4. When T small e.g., T=500, top_count=375, sample=125. If `np.argpartition(c, L-top_count)` requires kth within [0,L-1]. L-top_count >=1. Good.
If T=1, top_count = 1 - min(0,L)=1, sample=0. Good.

`build_active_matrix`:
```
def build_active_matrix(f, rows):
    n=f.size
    r_parts=[]; c_parts=[]; v_parts=[]
    for ridx in range(rows.size):
        k=int(rows[ridx])
        lo = k - n + 1
        if lo < 0: lo = 0
        hi = k
        if hi >= n: hi = n - 1
        if hi < lo: continue
        js = np.arange(lo, hi + 1, dtype=np.int32)
        vals = f[k - js]
        r = np.full(js.size, ridx, dtype=np.int32)
        r_parts.append(r); c_parts.append(js); v_parts.append(vals)
    if not r_parts:
       return None
    r_all=np.concatenate(r_parts); c_all=np.concatenate(c_parts); v_all=np.concatenate(v_parts)
    if v_all.size ==0: return None
    return _sp.csr_matrix((v_all, (r_all, c_all)), shape=(rows.size, n))
```
Rows size T; if some row no entries, matrix row all zero; still shape rows.size. For valid rows, no zero.

`lp_update`:
```
def lp_update(f, rows, M, upper):
    if not _HAVE_SCIPY: return None
    A=build_active_matrix(f, rows)
    if A is None: return None
    b_ub=np.full(rows.size, float(M), dtype=np.float64)
    c_obj=-np.ones(f.size, dtype=np.float64)
    try:
        res=_sco.linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=(0.0, float(upper)), method='highs', options={'presolve': True, 'time_limit': 120.0})
    except Exception:
        try:
            res=_sco.linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=(0.0, float(upper)), method='highs')
        except Exception:
            return None
    if res is None: return None
    x=getattr(res,'x',None)
    if x is None: return None
    b=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64)
    if b.size != f.size: return None
    b=np.where(b>0.0, b, 0.0)
    if not np.isfinite(b).all(): return None
    s=float(np.sum(b))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <=0: return None
    return b/s
```
Bounds tuple applies all variables? In linprog, if bounds is a tuple, it must be a 2-tuple (min,max) and applies to all? Documentation: bounds can be sequence of (min,max) or a single 2-sequence. I think `bounds=(0, None)` works. If not, we can create `bounds=[(0.0, float(upper))]*f.size` but memory okay. To be safe, use list? For n=7000, list of tuples overhead ~0.5MB, fine. Use `bounds = [(0.0, float(upper))] * n`. Let's do that to avoid ambiguity.
Time limit: if unsupported, second try without.

`refine_active_lp` as above. Need avoid while infinite. Use `it`.
```
def refine_active_lp(p, n, deadline, candidates, verbose=True):
    p=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    p=np.clip(p,0.0,None)
    s=p.sum()
    if not finite or s<=0: p=np.ones(n)/n
    else: p=p/s
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=fft_score(p); add_candidate(candidates,best_p)
    if verbose: print('LP start', best_score)
    L=2*n-1
    T=min(800,L)
    max_T=min(2500,L)
    upper=100.0/n + 0.5/np.sqrt(n)
    no_improve=0
    it=0
    last_report=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 120:  # leave time for exact
       it +=1
       c=fft_convolve_full(p)
       M=float(np.max(c))
       s=float(np.sum(p))
       cur_score=2.0*n*M/(s*s)
       if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_score=cur_score; best_p=p.copy(); add_candidate(candidates,best_p); no_improve=0
       else:
           no_improve +=1
       if verbose and it % 5 == 0:
           print(f'LP iter {it} score={cur_score:.6f} best={best_score:.6f} T={T}')
       if cur_score < 1.50 and it > 5: # if already great, could stop after stable
           # maybe continue a little? If best <1.50, good; but can still improve. We can break if <1.50 and no_improve>3
           if no_improve >3: break
       rows=select_active_rows(c,T)
       M_lp=M*(1.0+1e-7)+1e-14
       t0=time.monotonic()
       g=lp_update(p, rows, M_lp, upper)
       elapsed=time.monotonic()-t0
       if g is None:
           if T < max_T:
              T=min(max_T, int(T*1.5)+1); no_improve=0
           else:
              break
           continue
       best_alpha=0.0; best_alpha_score=cur_score
       for alpha in (0.5,0.3,0.2,0.1,0.05):
           h=(1.0-alpha)*p + alpha*g
           hs=h.sum()
           if not np.isfinite(hs) or hs <=0: continue
           h=h/hs
           sc=fft_score(h)
           if sc < best_alpha_score - 1e-13:
               best_alpha_score=sc; best_alpha=alpha
       if best_alpha == 0.0:
           no_improve +=1
           if no_improve > 4:
               if T < max_T:
                   T=min(max_T, int(T*1.5)+1); no_imprise? =0
               else:
                   break
       else:
           no_improve=0
           p=(1-best_alpha)*p + best_alpha*g
           p=p/np.sum(p)
           if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
              # best update already checked using p old; add new? The new p score best_alpha_score:
              if best_alpha_score < best_score:
                  best_score=best_alpha_score; best_p=p.copy(); add_candidate(candidates,best_p)
       if elapsed > 300 and T > 400:
          T=max(400, int(T*0.7))
    add_candidate(candidates,best_p)
    return best_p
```
Need update best after accepting alpha before or after set p. In code, after p assigned, check `best_alpha_score < best_score`.

```
def propose_candidate():
    start=time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + 1740.0
    parent = load_parent()
    if parent is not None and parent.size >=32 and parent.size <=20000:
        n=parent.size
    else:
        n=6962
    # If parent is None and maybe we want 6962.
    starts=[]
    profiles = [(1.0,0.0), (0.9,1.5), (0.8,1.0), (0.75,0.7), (0.95,2.0), (0.7,0.5)]
    for d,b in profiles:
       starts.append(make_profile(n,d,b))
    if parent is not None:
       ppar=np.clip(parent,0,1000)
       if ppar.sum()>0: starts.append(ppar/ppar.sum())
    best_start=None; best_start_score=inf
    for p in starts:
       sc=fft_score(p)
       if sc < best_start_score:
           best_start_score=sc; best_start=p.copy()
    print('start best', best_start_score)
    candidates=[]
    add_candidate(candidates, best_start)
    # exact evaluate starts (top maybe) to set exact best?
    # We can't store scores? Evaluate all starts (6) exact maybe okay.
    exact_seq=None; exact_score=inf
    for p in starts:
       seq=[float(x) for x in p]
       sc=safe_eval(seq)
       if sc < exact_score:
           exact_score=sc; exact_seq=seq
       add_candidate(candidates,p)
    if _HAVE_SCIPY and time.monotonic() < deadline - 10:
       best_start = refine_active_lp(best_start, n, deadline, candidates, verbose=True)
    # subgradient if time and score still high
    if best_start is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 60 and best_start_score > 1.60:
       best_start = subgradient_refine(best_start, n, deadline, candidates)
    # Final exact candidates
    # Add final best_start
    add_candidate(candidates, best_start)
    if exact_seq is None:
       exact_seq=[float(x) for x in best_start]; exact_score=safe_eval(exact_seq)
    # Exact evaluate candidates, respecting time
    for arr in candidates:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2: break
       seq=[float(x) for x in arr]
       sc=safe_eval(seq)
       if sc < exact_score:
           exact_score=sc; exact_seq=seq
    if exact_seq is None:
       # uniform fallback
       exact_seq=[1.0/n]*n
    return exact_seq
```

Let's write `subgradient_refine` top-level:
```
def subgradient_refine(p, n, deadline, candidates):
    p=...
    best=p.copy(); best_score=fft_score(p); add_candidate(candidates,best)
    if not np.isfinite(best_score): return best
    L=2*n-1
    top_k=min(200,L)
    eta=0.05
    failures=0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 20:
       it+=1
       c=fft_convolve_full(p)
       M=float(np.max(c)); s=float(np.sum(p)); score=2.0*n*M/(s*s)
       if score < best_score -1e-10:
           best=p.copy(); best_score=score; add_candidate(candidates,best); failures=0
       if best_score < 1.50 and it>20: break
       if top_k >= L: active=np.arange(L)
       else: active=np.argpartition(c, L-top_k)[-top_k:]
       d=np.zeros(n,dtype=np.float64)
       for k in active:
          k=int(k)
          lo=k-n+1
          if lo<0: lo=0
          hi=k+1
          if hi>n: hi=n
          if hi>lo:
             idx=np.arange(lo,hi,dtype=np.intp)
             d[idx] += p[k - idx]
          lo=k
          if lo<0: lo=0
          hi=k+n
          if hi>n: hi=n
          if hi>lo:
             idx2=np.arange(lo,hi,dtype=np.intp)
             d[idx2] += p[idx2 - k]
       scale=float(np.max(d)) if d.size else 0.0
       if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale <=0: break
       step=eta * d / scale
       # Clip step to avoid underflow
       if np.any(step < -3.0): step=np.maximum(step, -3.0)
       if np.any(step > 3.0): step=np.minimum(step, 3.0)
       p_new=p * np.exp(-step)
       p_new=np.maximum(p_new, 1e-15)
       snew=float(np.sum(p_new))
       if not np.isfinite(snew) or snew <=0: break
       p_new=p_new/snew
       new_score=fft_score(p_new)
       if new_score < score - 1e-12:
           p=p_new
           eta=min(eta*1.1, 0.5)
           failures=0
       else:
           eta=max(eta*0.6, 1e-4)
           failures+=1
           if failures>12: break
       if it % 20 ==0: print(...)
    return best
```

```
def load_parent_sequence():
    try:
       obj = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
    except Exception:
       return None
    if obj is None: return None
    try:
       arr=np.asarray(obj, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
       return None
    if arr.ndim !=1 or arr.size==0: return None
    arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(np.sum(arr))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=0: return None
    return arr/s
```

For weighted gradient with weights w_k for all k, derivative g_i = 2 Σ_{k: 0≤k-i<n} w_k p_{k-i}. This is 2 * cross-correlation of w and p. We can compute via FFT:

For real sequences a,b zero-padded length N circular, cross-correlation `corr[i] = Σ_j a[j] b[j+i]` (linear) if N large. We want C_i=Σ_j w_j p_{j-i}? Let set a=w, b=p. Using FFT: Circular correlation `corr = ifft(fft(w) * conj(fft(p)))` gives `corr[i] = Σ_j w_{(j+i) mod N} p_j`. This is C'_{i}=Σ_j w_{j+i} p_j. Our C_i=Σ_j w_j p_{j-i}. Let substitute m=j-i => j=m+i: C_i=Σ_m w_{m+i} p_m = corr[i]. Exactly! So C = circular correlation ifft(FFT(w)*conj(FFT(p))). With zero-padding enough (> L+n-1), linear correlation. Great. Algorithm `smoothed_gradient_step(p, weights_mode)`:
- c = fft_convolve_full(p)
- M = max(c)
- choose weights w: e.g., w=exp((c-M)/tau) for all k, or w=1 for top active.
- Compute correlation C = ifft(fft(w, N) * conj(fft(p, N))). Need N size large enough for linear correlation of w length L=2n-1 and p length n; max lag L-1, minimum? We need C_i for i=0..n-1 =Σ_j w_j p_{j-i}. This has support where j-i in [0,n-1]. The required circular convolution length at least (2n-1)+(n-1)=3n-2 to avoid wrap? Let's analyze. w length L=2n-1 (indices 0..L-1), p length n (0..n-1). C_i=Σ_j w_j p_{j-i}. For i=0..n-1, j ranges i..i+n-1. Max index i+n-1 ≤2n-2=L-1, min i≥0. So product indices within 0..L-1. The linear correlation can be computed by convolution of w_rev and p. To avoid circular aliasing, choose FFT size N ≥ L + n -1 = (2n-1)+n-1=3n-2. For n=7000, N=16384? 3n=21000, next power 32768. This is bigger than convolution size 16384 but still okay. If we use the same size as convolution, aliasing may corrupt. Use N=1; while N < 3*n-2: N<<=1. For n=6962, 3n-2=20884, N=32768. FFT of size 32768 twice per gradient; still fast (~0.01s). Great.

Let's implement a `gradient_descent_refine` using weighted correlation:
- p sum1.
- For iteration:
   c=fft_convolve_full(p) (size 16384)
   M=max c; score.
   Choose weights:
      Option A: softmax over all c with temperature `tau = max(1e-5, 0.05*M)`. w=exp((c-M)/tau). This weights near max smoothly. Sum w can be large; gradient scale by `w.sum()`.
      Option B: top active indicator with threshold `M - tol`. Use `w = np.where(c >= M - tol, 1.0, 0.0)`. If too many (>top_count), set top top_count to 1 (via argpartition). This yields sparse weights but FFT full size.
   Compute correlation C:
```
def weighted_gradient(p, w):
   n=p.size
   need = (w.size + p.size - 1)
   N=1
   while N < need: N <<=1
   W=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
   P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
   C=np.fft.irfft(W * np.conj(P), N)
   # C_i for i=0..n-1? Based on corr[i]=Σ_j w_{j+i} p_j. We need C_i=Σ_j w_j p_{j-i}. This is corr[i] with i? Let's test with small.
```
We need get the correct slice. Let's define linear correlation `R[i] = Σ_j w_j p_{j-i}` for i=0..n-1. We can compute by convolution of w with reversed p? Let's derive using FFT formula.
Circular correlation `corr = ifft(FFT(w) * conj(FFT(p)))` (zero-padded) returns for i=0..N-1: `corr[i] = Σ_j w[j] * p[(j - i) mod N]`? Let's check standard: Round? In numpy, `np.correlate(w,p)` full maybe corresponds to `ifft(fft(w)*conj(fft(p))).real` shifted. For circular correlation, `xcorr[n] = Σ_m x[m] conj(y[m+n])` in SciPy `correlate`? We need verify sign.
Let's test mentally small N large: w=[w0,w1], p=[p0,p1], zero-pad. `FFT(w)*conj(FFT(p))` inverse circular. Let compute via definition of FFT: F_w[m]=Σ_j w_j e^{-2πimj/N}, F_p=Σ_k p_k e^{-...}. Product inverse = (1/N)Σ_m Σ_jΣ_k w_j p_k e^{-2πimj} e^{+2πimk} e^{+2πimn} = Σ_j,k w_j p_k δ_{n + j - k ≡0} = Σ_j,k w_j p_k δ_{j ≡ k - n}. Thus value at n = Σ_k w_{k-n} p_k (since j=k-n). That is C'_{n}=Σ_k w_{k-n} p_k. We want C_i=Σ_j w_j p_{j-i}. Let k=j-i => j=k+i. Want Σ_k w_{k+i} p_k. That is value at n=-i mod N: n=N-i. So `corr[N-i] = Σ_k w_{k+i} p_k` for i>0, and corr[0]=Σ w_k p_k. Thus C_i for i=0..n-1 is at indices [N, N-1, ..., N-(n-1)] modulo N, i.e., `corr[(N - np.arange(n)) % N]`. Equivalently `corr[N-1::-1?` Let's test: i=0 index N mod N=0. i=1 index N-1. So slice `idx = (N - np.arange(n)) % N`. C=corr[idx].
Alternatively, compute `ifft(FFT(p) * conj(FFT(w)))` maybe gives direct order. Let's use the formula with index.
Let's verify with small exact using convolution. We can avoid sign uncertainty by testing with a simple vector mentally? Suppose w has only w_k=1 at k=K. Then C_i should be p_{K-i} if 0≤K-i<n else0. Our formula: corr at n=N-i = Σ_k w_{k-i}? Wait with only w_K, corr[n]=p_k if k-n=K => n=k-K. For i, n=N-i ≡ -i. Need k-K = -i => k=K-i. Yes, value p_{K-i}. Good. Index mapping works.
So gradient = 2*C for first n indices.

Thus `weighted_gradient(p,w)`:
```
def weighted_gradient(p,w):
    n=p.size
    need = w.size + n - 1
    N=1
    while N < need: N <<=1
    W=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    corr=np.fft.irfft(W * np.conj(P), N)
    idx = (N - np.arange(n)) % N
    g = 2.0 * corr[idx]
    g = np.maximum(g,0.0)?? derivative nonnegative; corr may small negative due FFT; clip.
    return g
```
If w is indicator, C might be large (number active * p_i). Scale step by max.

Let's design `gradient_refine` (gradient descent with weights):
```
def gradient_refine(p, n, deadline, candidates, use_softmax=True):
   p normalize
   best...
   eta=0.1
   tau_frac=0.05
   for it:
     c=fft_convolve_full(p); M=max c; score=...
     if improved update
     if best_score < 1.50 and it>20 and no_improve>... maybe break
     # weights
     if softmax:
        tau = max(1e-12, tau_frac * M)
        # To avoid underflow, w=exp((c-M)/tau)
        w=np.exp((c - M)/tau)
        # Maybe normalize w by sum to keep gradient scale stable
        wsum=w.sum()
        if finite wsum>0: w=w/wsum
        # Multiply by M? Gradient scale: if w normalized sum1, C ~ weighted average p ~1/n, small. We'll scale by max d.
     else:
        tol = max(1e-12, 0.02*M)
        active = np.where(c >= M - tol)[0]
        if active.size ==0: active=np.array([np.argmax(c)])
        if active.size > 200:
           # choose top 200 from active
           topk=200
           keep=np.argpartition(c[active], active.size-topk)[active.size-topk:]
           active=active[keep]
        w=np.zeros_like(c); w[active]=1.0
     g=weighted_gradient(p,w)
     scale=max(g)
     if scale<=0: break
     step=eta * g/scale
     # Mix multiplicative and additive? Multiplicative may slow. Use p_new = p - step*eta? But nonneg.
     # Since p small, multiplicative okay.
     p_new = p * np.exp(-step)
     p_new = np.clip(p_new,1e-15,None); normalize
     new_score=fft_score(p_new)
     if new_score < score -1e-12:
        p=p_new; eta=min(eta*1.02,0.5); no_improve=0
     else:
        eta=max(eta*0.7,1e-4); no_improve++
        if no_improve>20: break
     if it%50 print
   return best
```

Let's implement `weighted_gradient` carefully:
```
def weighted_gradient(p, w):
    n = p.size
    need = w.size + n - 1
    size=1
    while size < need: size <<= 1
    W = np.fft.rfft(w, size)
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, size)
    corr = np.fft.irfft(W * np.conj(P), size)
    idx = (size - np.arange(n)) % size
    g = 2.0 * corr[idx]
    # corr may be slightly negative for small true values
    g[g < 0.0] = 0.0
    return g
```
If w normalized, g values ~? good.

Let's test derivative with small example mentally to ensure index. Let n=2, p=[p0,p1], w only k=1. c1=2p0p1. Gradient wrt p0=2p1, wrt p1=2p0. Our w length 3? Actually c length 3, w[1]=1. need=3+2-1=4, size4. corr[n]=Σ_k w_{k-n} p_k. We want idx for i0: (4-0)%4=0. corr[0]=Σ_k w_k p_k = w0p0+w1p1+w2p2= p1. i1: idx=3. corr[3]=Σ_k w_{k-3 mod4}p_k. k=0 => w_{1}p0=p0; k=1=>w2p1=0. corr3=p0. g=2[p1,p0]. correct. Good.

What about w only k=0: gradient c0=p0^2 -> [2p0,0]. idx i0=0: corr0=w0p0=p0. i1=3: corr3 = w_{k-3}: k=0 w1=0, k=1 w0? w0 index? For N=4,k=1 => w_{-2}=w2=0; no p1. Good. g [2p0,0]. Good.

```
def gradient_refine(p, n, deadline, candidates, verbose=True):
    p=normalize
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=fft_score(p); add_candidate(...)
    eta=0.2
    tau_frac=0.05
    no_improve=0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 30:
       it+=1
       c=fft_convolve_full(p)
       M=float(np.max(c)); s=float(np.sum(p)); score=2*n*M/(s*s)
       if score < best_score -1e-11:
          best_score=score; best_p=p.copy(); add_candidate(candidates,best_p); no_improve=0
       else: no_improve+=1
       if verbose and it%100==0: print(...)
       if no_improve > 40:
          # maybe change tau/eta: if score not improving, try larger step or different tau
          if no_improve == 41:
             tau_frac = max(0.01, tau_frac*0.5)
             eta = min(eta*2.0,1.0)
          if no_improve > 80: break
       tau = max(1e-14, tau_frac * M)
       w=np.exp((c-M)/tau)
       ws=float(np.sum(w))
       if not finite or ws<=0: break
       w=w/ws
       g=weighted_gradient(p,w)
       scale=float(np.max(g))
       if not finite or scale<=0: break
       step=eta*g/scale
       # clip step
       np.minimum(step, 3.0, out=step); np.maximum(step, -3.0, out=step)
       p_new=p*np.exp(-step)
       p_new=np.maximum(p_new, 1e-15)
       snew=float(np.sum(p_new))
       if not finite or snew<=0: break
       p_new=p_new/snew
       new_score=fft_score(p_new)
       if new_score < score -1e-12:
          p=p_new
          eta=min(eta*1.03, 1.0)
       else:
          eta=max(eta*0.7, 1e-3)
          if eta < 1e-3 and no_improve>5: break? maybe
    add_candidate(candidates,best_p)
    return best_p
```

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative coefficient vector minimizing
    2*n*max(convolve(a, a)) / (sum(a)**2).

The program uses:
  * strong analytic starts (left-singular step profiles),
  * fast FFT-based gradient / mirror-descent iterations on a smoothed
    maximum-convolution objective,
  * a relaxed SOTA-style linear-programming update on the most active
    convolution constraints when SciPy is available,
  * final exact scoring through the provided evaluate_sequence().
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    import scipy.optimize as _sco
    import scipy.sparse as _sp
    _HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    _HAVE_SCIPY = False


def load_parent_sequence():
    """Return the supplied height_sequence_1 as a normalized non-negative vector."""
    try:
        obj = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
    except Exception:
        return None
    if obj is None:
        return None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(obj, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return None
    if arr.ndim != 1 or arr.size == 0:
        return None
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    return arr / s


def fft_convolve_full(a):
    """Full linear convolution a*a using FFT."""
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    need = 2 * n - 1
    size = 1
    while size < need:
        size <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, size)
    conv = np.asarray(conv, dtype=np.float64)[:need]
    conv[conv < 0.0] = 0.0
    return conv


def fft_score(p):
    """Fast proxy for evaluate_sequence() for a normalized vector."""
    n = int(p.size)
    if n <= 0:
        return float("inf")
    c = fft_convolve_full(p)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    maxc = float(np.max(c))
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if not np.isfinite(maxc) or not np.isfinite(s) or s == 0.0:
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * maxc / (s * s))


def safe_eval(seq):
    """Call the official evaluator with exception and validity guards."""
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")
    try:
        val = float(val)
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return float("inf")
    return val


def add_candidate(candidates, arr, limit=36):
    """Normalize and store a candidate in a fixed-size FIFO list."""
    if candidates is None:
        return
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return
    if arr.ndim != 1 or arr.size == 0:
        return
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, None)
    if not np.isfinite(arr).all():
        return
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return
    arr = arr / s
    candidates.append(arr.copy())
    while len(candidates) > limit:
        candidates.pop(0)


def make_profile(n, delta, b):
    """
    A left-singular profile:
        f(x) = x^{-1/2} for x < delta, f(x) = b otherwise.
    """
    x = (np.arange(n) + 0.5) / float(n)
    w = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    mask = x < delta
    w[mask] = 1.0 / np.sqrt(x[mask])
    w[~mask] = float(b)
    s = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        w = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        w = w / s
    return w


def make_exp_profile(n, lam):
    """Left-singular profile modulated by exp(lam*x)."""
    x = (np.arange(n) + 0.5) / float(n)
    w = np.exp(lam * x) / np.sqrt(x)
    s = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        w = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        w = w / s
    return w


def select_active_rows(c, T):
    """Select a set of convolution lags likely to be active."""
    L = c.size
    if T <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.int64)
    if T >= L:
        return np.arange(L, dtype=np.int64)

    top_count = int(T - min(T // 4, L))
    if top_count < 1:
        top_count = 1
    if top_count > L:
        top_count = L

    if top_count >= L:
        top_idx = np.arange(L, dtype=np.int64)
    else:
        top_idx = np.argpartition(c, L - top_count)[L - top_count:]

    sample_count = T - top_count
    if sample_count > L:
        sample_count = L
    if sample_count > 0:
        sample_idx = np.linspace(0, L - 1, sample_count).astype(np.int64)
    else:
        sample_idx = np.array([], dtype=np.int64)

    rows = np.unique(np.concatenate((top_idx, sample_idx)))
    if rows.size > T:
        vals = c[rows]
        if rows.size - T <= 0:
            keep_idx = np.arange(rows.size)
        else:
            keep_idx = np.argpartition(vals, rows.size - T)[rows.size - T:]
        rows = rows[keep_idx]
    return rows


def weighted_gradient(p, w):
    """
    Exact gradient of sum_k w_k (p*p)_k:
        d_k = 2 * sum_j w_j p_{j-k},  k=0..n-1.
    Computed by one zero-padded FFT cross-correlation.
    """
    n = p.size
    need = w.size + n - 1
    size = 1
    while size < need:
        size <<= 1
    W = np.fft.rfft(w, size)
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, size)
    corr = np.fft.irfft(W * np.conj(P), size)
    idx = (size - np.arange(n)) % size
    g = 2.0 * corr[idx]
    g[g < 0.0] = 0.0
    return g


def gradient_refine(p, n, deadline, candidates, verbose=True):
    """
    Fast mirror-descent / multiplicative gradient method on a softmax-smoothed
    maximum convolution coefficient.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, None)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    else:
        p = p / s

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = fft_score(p)
    add_candidate(candidates, best_p)
    if verbose:
        print("gradient start score = %.8f" % best_score)

    eta = 0.18
    tau_frac = 0.045
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 25.0:
        it += 1
        c = fft_convolve_full(p)
        M = float(np.max(c))
        s = float(np.sum(p))
        cur_score = float(2.0 * n * M / (s * s))

        if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = cur_score
            best_p = p.copy()
            add_candidate(candidates, best_p)
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        if verbose and (it == 1 or it % 100 == 0):
            print("grad iter %d cur=%.8f best=%.8f eta=%.4f tauFrac=%.4f"
                  % (it, cur_score, best_score, eta, tau_frac))

        if no_improve > 50 and no_improve != 51:
            pass
        if no_improve == 51:
            tau_frac = max(0.012, tau_frac * 0.6)
            eta = min(1.0, eta * 1.8)
        if no_improve == 101:
            tau_frac = max(0.008, tau_frac * 0.7)
            eta = min(1.0, eta * 1.4)
        if no_improve > 180:
            break

        tau = max(1e-14, tau_frac * M)
        w = np.exp((c - M) / tau)
        ws = float(np.sum(w))
        if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
            break
        w = w / ws

        g = weighted_gradient(p, w)
        scale = float(np.max(g))
        if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale <= 0.0:
            break

        step = (eta * g) / scale
        np.minimum(step, 3.0, out=step)
        np.maximum(step, -3.0, out=step)

        p_new = p * np.exp(-step)
        p_new = np.maximum(p_new, 1e-16)
        s2 = float(np.sum(p_new))
        if not np.isfinite(s2) or s2 <= 0.0:
            break
        p_new = p_new / s2

        new_score = fft_score(p_new)
        if np.isfinite(new_score) and new_score < cur_score - 1e-13:
            p = p_new
            eta = min(1.0, eta * 1.04)
        else:
            eta = max(1e-3, eta * 0.75)
            if eta < 2e-3 and no_improve > 8:
                break

    add_candidate(candidates, best_p)
    if verbose:
        print("gradient end best=%.8f" % best_score)
    return best_p


def build_active_matrix(f, rows):
    """Build the selected constraint matrix for f*b <= M."""
    n = f.size
    r_parts = []
    c_parts = []
    v_parts = []

    for ridx in range(rows.size):
        k = int(rows[ridx])
        lo = k - n + 1
        if lo < 0:
            lo = 0
        hi = k
        if hi >= n:
            hi = n - 1
        if hi < lo:
            continue
        js = np.arange(lo, hi + 1, dtype=np.int32)
        vals = f[k - js]
        r = np.full(js.size, ridx, dtype=np.int32)
        r_parts.append(r)
        c_parts.append(js)
        v_parts.append(vals)

    if not v_parts:
        return None

    r_all = np.concatenate(r_parts)
    c_all = np.concatenate(c_parts)
    v_all = np.concatenate(v_parts)
    if v_all.size == 0:
        return None

    return _sp.csr_matrix((v_all, (r_all, c_all)), shape=(rows.size, n))


def lp_update(f, rows, M, upper):
    """
    Solve the relaxed SOTA LP:
        maximize sum(b)
        s.t. selected rows of f*b <= M, 0 <= b <= upper.
    """
    if not _HAVE_SCIPY:
        return None
    A = build_active_matrix(f, rows)
    if A is None:
        return None

    b_ub = np.full(rows.size, float(M), dtype=np.float64)
    c_obj = -np.ones(f.size, dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, float(upper))] * f.size

    try:
        res = _sco.linprog(
            c_obj,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b_ub,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options={"presolve": True, "time_limit": 120.0}
        )
    except Exception:
        try:
            res = _sco.linprog(
                c_obj,
                A_ub=A,
                b_ub=b_ub,
                bounds=bounds,
                method="highs"
            )
        except Exception:
            return None

    if res is None:
        return None
    x = getattr(res, "x", None)
    if x is None:
        return None

    b = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if b.size != f.size:
        return None
    b = np.where(b > 0.0, b, 0.0)
    if not np.isfinite(b).all():
        return None
    s = float(np.sum(b))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    return b / s


def refine_active_lp(p, n, deadline, candidates, verbose=True):
    """
    Relaxed SOTA update: solve an LP using the most active convolution
    constraints, then take a line-search step toward the LP solution.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, None)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    else:
        p = p / s

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = fft_score(p)
    add_candidate(candidates, best_p)
    if verbose:
        print("LP start score = %.8f" % best_score)

    L = 2 * n - 1
    T = min(900, L)
    max_T = min(2800, L)
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 35.0:
        it += 1
        c = fft_convolve_full(p)
        M = float(np.max(c))
        s = float(np.sum(p))
        cur_score = float(2.0 * n * M / (s * s))

        if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = cur_score
            best_p = p.copy()
            add_candidate(candidates, best_p)
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        if verbose and (it == 1 or it % 5 == 0):
            print("LP iter %d cur=%.8f best=%.8f T=%d"
                  % (it, cur_score, best_score, T))

        rows = select_active_rows(c, T)
        if rows.size == 0:
            break

        M_lp = M * (1.0 + 1e-8) + 1e-14
        upper = max(100.0 / float(n) + 0.5 / np.sqrt(float(n)),
                    2.2 * float(np.max(p)))

        t0 = time.monotonic()
        g = lp_update(p, rows, M_lp, upper)
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - t0

        if g is None:
            if T < max_T:
                T = min(max_T, int(T * 1.4) + 1)
                no_improve = 0
            else:
                break
            continue

        best_alpha = 0.0
        best_alpha_score = cur_score
        for alpha in (0.55, 0.35, 0.22, 0.12, 0.06):
            h = (1.0 - alpha) * p + alpha * g
            hs = float(np.sum(h))
            if not np.isfinite(hs) or hs <= 0.0:
                continue
            h = h / hs
            sc = fft_score(h)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_alpha_score - 1e-13:
                best_alpha_score = sc
                best_alpha = alpha

        if best_alpha == 0.0:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 4:
                if T < max_T:
                    T = min(max_T, int(T * 1.5) + 1)
                    no_improve = 0
                else:
                    break
        else:
            p = (1.0 - best_alpha) * p + best_alpha * g
            ps = float(np.sum(p))
            if not np.isfinite(ps) or ps <= 0.0:
                break
            p = p / ps
            no_improve = 0
            if best_alpha_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_score = best_alpha_score
                best_p = p.copy()
                add_candidate(candidates, best_p)

        if elapsed > 260.0 and T > 500:
            T = max(500, int(T * 0.7))

    add_candidate(candidates, best_p)
    if verbose:
        print("LP end best=%.8f" % best_score)
    return best_p


def propose_candidate():
    """
    Return a list of non-negative coefficients. Finds strong analytic starts,
    refines them with FFT gradient descent and a relaxed LP, and returns the
    best exact-scored candidate found.
    """
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    final_deadline = t0 + 1770.0

    parent = load_parent_sequence()
    if parent is not None and 256 <= parent.size <= 15000:
        n = int(parent.size)
    else:
        n = 6962

    starts = []

    profile_specs = [
        (1.0, 0.0),
        (0.9, 1.2),
        (0.8, 1.0),
        (0.75, 0.8),
        (0.95, 1.8),
        (0.7, 0.6),
        (0.85, 1.4),
    ]
    for d, b in profile_specs:
        starts.append(make_profile(n, d, b))

    lam_specs = [0.0, 0.4, 0.9, -0.35, -0.8]
    for lam in lam_specs:
        starts.append(make_exp_profile(n, lam))

    if parent is not None:
        starts.append(parent)

    candidates = []
    best_start = None
    best_start_score = float("inf")

    for p in starts:
        p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
        add_candidate(candidates, p)
        sc = fft_score(p)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_start_score:
            best_start_score = sc
            best_start = p.copy()

    if best_start is None:
        best_start = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        best_start_score = fft_score(best_start)
        add_candidate(candidates, best_start)

    print("initial best fft score = %.8f" % best_start_score)

    # Fast gradient phase.
    grad_deadline_1 = t0 + 650.0
    if time.monotonic() < grad_deadline_1 - 20.0:
        best_start = gradient_refine(best_start, n, grad_deadline_1, candidates,
                                     verbose=True)

    cur_score = fft_score(best_start)
    if not np.isfinite(cur_score):
        cur_score = float("inf")

    # Relaxed LP phase, mainly if gradient has not yet crossed the benchmark.
    lp_deadline = t0 + 1350.0
    if (_HAVE_SCIPY and cur_score > 1.503 and time.monotonic() < lp_deadline - 30.0):
        best_start = refine_active_lp(best_start, n, lp_deadline, candidates,
                                      verbose=True)
        cur_score = fft_score(best_start)
        if not np.isfinite(cur_score):
            cur_score = float("inf")

    # Final gradient polish.
    grad2_deadline = min(final_deadline - 90.0, time.monotonic() + 320.0)
    if time.monotonic() < grad2_deadline - 20.0:
        best_start = gradient_refine(best_start, n, grad2_deadline, candidates,
                                     verbose=False)

    add_candidate(candidates, best_start)

    # Exact scoring of the retained candidate pool.
    best_seq = [float(x) for x in best_start]
    best_exact = safe_eval(best_seq)

    for arr in candidates:
        if time.monotonic() > final_deadline - 4.0:
            break
        seq = [float(x) for x in arr]
        sc = safe_eval(seq)
        if sc < best_exact:
            best_exact = sc
            best_seq = seq

    if not np.isfinite(best_exact):
        best_seq = [1.0 / float(n)] * n

    return best_seq
```
